## Pagina 2

Author's Note

This book was designed to meet the needs of the beginner and professional

guitarist for a complete and thorough guide to GUITAR CHORDS. The chord forms

are presented in each key and are sectioned into “melody chords", 
“inside chords",

“thythm chords", and “bottom 4-string chords". In actual practice, the usage of

these forms is interchangeable. One might use certain “melody chord forms’ while

playing rhythm or certain “rhythm chord forms" for melody or solo playing. These

categories serve as an essential aid in memorization and in knowing generally the

best usage of a particular chord form. Mastering this text will give the guitarist firm

command over the thousands of voicings and chord forms found throughout the

fingerboard. We believe it to be the most complete and thorough guitar chord book

ever assembled.

William Bay

Copyright 1971 Mel Bay Publications, Inc.
International Copyright Secured 
Printed in U.S.A.

## Pagina 5

Tuning the Guitar

The six open strings of the guitar will be of the same pitch as the six notes shown in the illustration

of the piano keyboard. Note that five of the strings are below the middle C of the piano keyboard.

[DIAGRAMA: imagini_partituri/diagrama-p5-C.png | PUNCTE: 1/1 3/1 3/5 4/1 5/1 5/5]

1. Tune the 6th string in unison to the E or twelfth white key

to the LEFT of MIDDLE C on the piano.

2. Place the finger behind the fifth fret of the 6th string. This
will give you the tone or pitch of the 5th string. (A)

3. Place finger behind the fifth fret of the 5th string to get the

pitch of the 4th string. (D)

4. Repeat same procedure to obtain the pitch of the 3rd

string. (G)

5. Place finger behind the FOURTH FRET of the 3rd string to

get the pitch of the 2nd string. (B)

6. Place finger behind the fifth fret of the 2nd string to get the

pitch of the Ist string. 
(E)

Pitch Pipes

Pitch pipes with instructions for their usage may be obtained at any music

store. Each pipe will have 
the correct pitch of each guitar string and are

recommended to be used when a piano is not available.

## Pagina 145

Building Right Hand Technique

BURDING
RIGHT, 
HAND
TECHNIQUE

Building Right Hand Technique is a master technical study book for the
use of a flat pick. The studies are designed to build CLEANNESS and
CLARITY of sound. Many of the studies are designed to develop ease
and agility in playing rapid passages which contain skips from string
to string. Other studies are designed to develop right hand picking

speed on linear passages. The material contained in this text consists
of original studies, works of the great classical guitar masters arranged
for plectrum guitar, fiddle tunes, jazz etudes and many other studies

in varied styles.

---
